Alternative: use numerical optimization with-exp methods from good initialization. Need likely enough to reach 1.5027. Let's design robust optimizer.

Objective for normalized p (sum=1): f(p)=max_k C_k. Want minimize. We can use smooth approximation: minimize F_T(p)=T log(∑ exp((C_k - offset)/T)) (log-sum-exp) which approximates max, or minimize ∑ C_k^q for large q (L_q norm), which promotes smaller max and might find flatter. Since C_k nonnegative. Use gradient? We can use coordinate/block multiplicative updates.

Parameterization: maintain log p (simplex). Use softmax: p_i = exp(θ_i)/Z. We can use gradient descent on F(p) w.r.t θ. Gradient w.r.t θ_i = [∂F/∂p_i (Z p_i) - p_i ∑ p_j ∂F/∂p_j] (centered). For F=∑ C_k^q, ∂C_k/∂p_i = p_{k-i} (valid). So ∂F/∂p_i = 2q ∑_k C_k^{q-1} p_{k-i}. This can be computed as convolution of p with weights w_k = 2q C_k^{q-1}. Specifically g_i = (p * w)[i]? Let p normalized S=1. C = p*p. We want minimize max C. In DFT, if P(ω) = ∑ p_i e^{-iω i}, then DFT of C (length 2n-1) is P(ω) P(ω) = P(ω)^2 (with appropriate no conjugate). Algorithm for improvement cycles:
1. Sort candidates. For each candidate, perform a number of iterations (time-limited) with random parameter variants:
   - compute C, M.
   - choose weights: w = softmax((C-M)/T) with T in [0.02,0.2]*M? Or polynomial r^q.
   - compute g.
   - generate p_update = p * exp(-η g) normalized; and also maybe p_update = p * (1 + η*(g_min - g))? Use exp.
   - perform line search: for λ in [0,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0], p_test=(1-λ)p+λ p_update, normalize, score. Pick best if <best.
   - If improved, set p=best; else try smaller η or different T.
2. To avoid local minima, periodically perturb best: random block with larger width, or blend with a different parametric candidate.

        g = solve_lp(f, M, lp_limit)

            M=best_score
            last_improve=now

            # maybe try smaller t or derived from LP: t= (T/sumg)-? If LP suggests g, optimal t maybe 1? If no improvement, break or try random perturb
            break
        count+=1
    return f, M
```

Smooth update step:
```
def smooth_step(p, C, g, eta):
   # g centered for stability
   gm = g - g.mean()
   # scale to max abs 1
   scale = max(1e-15, np.max(np.abs(gm)))
   y = p * np.exp(-eta * gm / scale)
   y = np.maximum(y, 1e-30)
   y /= y.sum()
   return y
```
But if eta*scale=eta, good. If gm range not centered? We use gm/scale. For initial central, gm for small i positive (since high g) and tail negative? Since mean maybe around? p0/g high, tail g low, mean maybe ~? Centering makes tail positive exponent, boosting tail. Good.

We can also use uncentered with exp(-eta*g/scale), which multiplies all by varying; normalization equivalent to centering plus constant? Centering removes common factor; for nonlinearity, same after normalization because exp(-eta(g-mean)/scale)=exp(-eta g/scale)*exp(+eta mean/scale). So center exactly not change p_new. Good for stability.

Line search for lambda? We are already applying full eta. Could try multiple eta values [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1,2]. Pick best. This is a directional step. If none improves, try different q.

Parametric search can provide candidate shapes. Let's define `_parametric_p(n, alpha, beta, gamma, delta=0)`:
```
i=np.arange(n)
left = i + 1
right = n - i
p = np.power(left, -alpha) + gamma * np.power(right, -beta) + delta*np.ones(n)
p=np.maximum(p,0)
if p.sum()==0: p=np.ones(n)
return p/p.sum()
```
Parameters alpha, beta in [0,0.9], gamma in [0,5], delta in [0,1]. We can do random search around central (alpha=0.5,beta=0,gamma=0) and around maybe alpha=0.3. But maybe smooth/LP enough.

Let's implement a self-contained LP solver with fallback to a greedy LP approximation if linprog unavailable or too slow.

Greedy LP approximation:
Given f,M. We want a feasible g with large sum. Start g=f (feasible). While time, find column j with best marginal: The maximum allowable increase δ_j such that constraints not violated: for each row k in j..j+n-1, slack = M - (A g)_k; coefficient f_{k-j}. δ_j = min_k slack / f_{k-j}. Increase g_j by δ_j (or fraction). This is a greedy packing algorithm; it maximizes sum by adding to variables with smallest blocking. Since constraints linear and objective uniform, this is like a primal-dual. It may converge to near LP optimum. It's O(n²) per step if recompute slacks; but can update. We can do a few steps using FFT to compute violations and find best j? Need min over rows of slack/coeff. We can approximate by adding small uniform or use multiplicative. Maybe not needed if linprog works.

But if linprog not available, multiplicative smooth update can still improve.

Let's implement upsample:
```
def upsample(p, n_new):
   m=len(p)
   if m==n_new: return p
   x_old=np.arange(m)
   x_new=np.linspace(0, m-1, n_new)
   p_new=np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
   p_new=np.maximum(p_new,0); p_new/=p_new.sum(); return p_new
```
Interpolation of asymmetric sequence with endpoint singular (first value high) may smooth endpoint; okay.

Could also map by ratio: x_new = (np.arange(n_new)*(m-1))/(n_new-1). Same.

After upsample, normalize to T for LP.

We can return best found globally (any length). If best from n=1024 has lower score than n=2535? Score target independent; if it's lower, return length1024. But problem says larger sequences often better; however evaluator accepts any. We should return best score. If a low-res sequence gets 1.50 and high-res not, return low-res. But target upper bound maybe expects? No rule fixed length. OK.

LP build A:
```
def _build_A(f, n):
   diagonals=[]
   for d in range(n):
       diagonals.append(np.full(n, f[d], dtype=np.float64))
   offsets=list(range(n))
   return diags(diagonals, offsets, shape=(2*n-1, n), format='csr')
```

Solve LP:
```
def _lp_direction(f, M, time_limit):
   n=len(f); T=np.sqrt(2*n)
   A=_build_A(f,n)
   b=M*(1+1e-9)+1e-12
   b=np.full(2*n-1,b)
   c=-np.ones(n)
   options={'presolve': True, 'output_flag': 0, 'time_limit': max(1.0, float(time_limit))}
   res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0, np.inf), method='highs', options=options)
   if res.x is not None:
       g=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
       g=np.maximum(g,0)
       if np.isfinite(g).all() and g.sum()>0:
           # check feasibility approximately
           # violations = A.dot(g) but A may be huge; use conv f*g
           # g length n, f length n; conv via fft
           vg = _convolve(f, g)  # need function general lin conv
           if np.max(vg) <= M*1.05 + 1e-8:
               return g
   return None
```
Need `_convolve_general(a,b)`: full conv length 2n-1 using FFT. For LP feasibility check can skip or approximate; if infeasible, line search will handle? But acceptable. Include for safety.

`linprog` may not support `time_limit` in options for HiGHS? I think yes. If not, KeyError? Wrap: try options with time_limit; if fails, without. Also `method='highs'` may not be available in old scipy; fallback `method='interior-point'` with dense? Not for large. We'll assume modern.

Could use `res.status` 0 optimal, 3 time limit with solution? We'll use res.x if success or res.status==3 and x finite. But if time limit, x may not satisfy bounds? clip.

LP time limit: We don't want one LP to consume too much. We can pass `lp_budget = min(30, remaining*0.2)` for small n, but if no solution within, fallback. For larger n, if solve >60s, maybe too slow. We can set max 60s initially and adapt: If a solve returns quickly, increase; if times out often, decrease or skip larger n.

Block local polish:
```
def _local_polish(p, budget, rng):
  best=p; best_score
  n=len(p)
  widths=[int(max(1,n*frac)) for frac in [0.002,0.01,0.03,0.1]]
  while time:
    width=choice(widths)
    start=random int
    cand=p.copy()
    sigma=choice([0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5])
    cand[start:start+width]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,width))
    # every so often add small gaussian to whole
    cand=np.maximum(cand,0); normalize
    s=score(cand)
    if s<best: accept; else maybe reduce sigma
```

The primal LP solution can be approximated by multiplicative weights (MPW) as follows for packing LP max 1ᵀx s.t. Ax≤1, x≥0. There is an algorithm:
Initialize x_i=1/m maybe. For t=1..T:
  For each constraint k, let violation v_k = (Ax)_k.
  For each variable i, x_i ← x_i * (1 - ε ∑_k A_{ki} v_k / (max v +1))
Normalize? This tends to an approximate optimum. But we need a single g scaling sum? We can run for a few iterations starting x=1/n. This is exactly similar to smooth update but on g. It may be faster than LP and can serve as direction. However, solving LP with exact may not be necessary; a few MPW iterations could produce good g and update f. We can implement `_approx_lp_direction(f,M,budget)` using FFT to compute A g each iteration and update g.

Let's derive an efficient MPW for primal LP:
We want maximize sum g subject to A g ≤ M. Scale constraints to ≤1 by dividing M: B=A/M. Let Feasible g with Bg≤1. Initialize g = f/M? Since f*f≤M => B f ≤1, and sum f=T. Or uniform? Use f as start.
MPW for packing (max weight): x_i^{new} = x_i * (1 - η ∑_k B_{ki} z_k) where z_k = (B x)_k / max_k (B x)_k maybe. But to maintain feasibility, after iteration scale x by 1/max_k (Bx) to enforce ≤1. Objective sum increases? Let's try:
- x feasible (Bx≤1).
- Compute violations v=Bx (≤1).
- Update y_i = x_i * (1 - ε ∑_k B_{ki} v_k)
- Scale y by 1/max_k (By) to re-feasible (if max>0).
This is a multiplicative weight algorithm for maximum weight independent set; it should improve sum? Not guaranteed but heuristic. The weights for variables are those contributing to high-violation constraints. This is analogous to LP dual variables. It may produce a good g.

We can implement this approximate LP direction instead of/in addition to linprog. It uses FFT each iteration: given g, compute v = conv(f/M,g) (length 2n-1), then gradient h_i = ∑_k B_{ki} v_k = (1/M)∑ f_{k-i} v_k. This is a correlation of f and v, similar to gradient. We can compute with FFT: h = conv? B_{k,i}=f_{k-i}/M. h_i = (1/M)∑_{j=0}^{n-1} f_j v_{i+j}. This is same as gradient formula with p=f_j and w=v/M. We have `_grad_weights(f, v/M)` returns h_i. Then update y = x * (1 - ε * max(0,h?)) but y must nonnegative. Use exp(-η h normalized). Then scale to feasible: compute v_y=conv(f/M,y); maxv; if maxv>1, y/=maxv. Then sum_y. The LP direction should have large sum; choose y if sum≥T.

This approximate direction may be much faster than linprog and maybe as effective for update. The SOTA's LP direction can be approximated by running many MPW iterations starting from f. We can allocate time to produce g. Then line search mix f with g. This is robust even without scipy. Let's implement both: try exact LP if available and fast; else use MPW approximate. Or use MPW always as part of smooth? The LP update differs: It optimizes g under current f; the update f←mix(g) is like gradient ascent on sum under bound. Our smooth update updates f directly with weights from f*f. MPW g update might be more powerful.

Let's formulate a function `best_response_approx(f, M, iters/time)`:

while time<max_time and no<30:
   # compute h from viol (current tight)
   h = _grad_weights(f, viol) / M
   h = h - h.mean()
   sc = np.max(np.abs(h))
   if sc <=1e-300: break
   y = x * np.exp(-eta * (h/sc))
   y = np.maximum(y, 0)
   # scale y to tight feasible
   vy = _conv_general(f, y)
   maxY = vy.max()
   if maxY <=0: break
   y = y / (maxY / M)  # since want conv/M max 1: y_new = y * M/maxY = y / (maxY/M)
   # equivalently y = y * M / maxY
   s = y.sum()
   if s > best_sum*(1+1e-10):
       best_x=y; best_sum=s; x=y; no=0; eta = min(eta*1.2, 6.0)
   else:
       no +=1
       x=best_x
       if eta > 0.5: eta*=0.7
       else: eta*=1.5 (try larger)
       # recompute viol for x? Need viol for next loop. We can set viol = _conv_general(f,x)/M; max should be 1 maybe; scale.
       viol = _conv_general(f,x)/M; maxv=viol.max(); if maxv>0: viol=viol/maxv
       if no>=20: break
   # if a max constraint not tight due scaling? we set y *M/maxY so tight.
return best_x if best_sum > T*(1+1e-6) else None
```
This uses one conv for vy per iter, grad_weights uses FFT (2 FFTs) for h. Could be ~3 conv/iter. But `h = grad_weights(f, viol) / M`: `grad_weights` expects w length 2n-1 and p length n. It computes ∑ p_j w_{i+j}. Here p=f_j, w=viol. Correct. Divide by M; if M in score? For LP constraints A g ≤ M, B=A/M; h_i=∑(f_{k-i}/M) v_k? v=conv/M? Wait v_k = (f*x)_k / M. In MPW, violation should be v_k (constraint left/M). Update coefficient ∑ A_{k,i} v_k / M? Actually if constraints Bx≤1 with B_{k,i}=A_{k,i}/M=f_{k-i}/M. The "dual" gradient is ∑ B_{k,i} v_k = (1/M)∑ f_{k-i} v_k. Since v=conv/M, this equals (1/M)∑ f_{k-i} * (conv_k/M) = ∑ f ... / M^2. But for MPW, the exact exponent weight should use A_{ki} * y_k/M? Let's derive.

We want update x_i based on constraints: new x_i = x_i * (1 - η ∑_k B_{ki} v_k), where v_k = (B x)_k (≤1). B_{ki}=A_{ki}/M = f_{k-i}/M. So weight w_i = ∑_k (f_{k-i}/M) v_k = (1/M)∑ f_{k-i} v_k. Our `grad_weights(f, v)` gives ∑ f_{j} v_{i+j}, with j=k-i, exactly ∑ f_{k-i} v_k. Then divide by M. So h = grad/M. Good. v already dimensionless (Bx). We do not need to divide by M again? Let's define `_sota_loop` detailed:
```
def _sota_loop(p, budget, allow_lp=False):
    n=len(p); T=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    f = normalize(p,T)
    M = _score(f)
    g = f.copy()
    start=time.monotonic(); last_improve=start; last_lp=0
    it=0
    while time.monotic()-start < budget:
       rem=budget - elapsed
       if rem < 3: break
       # choose direction time: min(15, rem*0.5) for approx; exact occasionally uses up to 30
       dir_time = min(10.0, max(0.5, rem*0.4))
       g_new=None
       if allow_lp and (it % 3 == 0) and rem > 60:
           t_lim = min(30.0, rem*0.3)
           g_new = _lp_direction(f, M, t_lim)
       if g_new is None:
           g_new = _approx_best_response(f, M, g, dir_time)
       it += 1
       if g_new is None:
           # try longer once? maybe if rem big
           if rem > 60:
              g_new = _approx_best_response(f,M,g, min(30, rem*0.5))
           if g_new is None:
              break
       s_g_sum = np.sum(g_new)
       if s_g_sum <= T:
           # scale? If <T cannot improve sum? LP optimum maybe no.
           continue? break after few
       g_scaled = g_new * (T / s_g_sum)
       # line search
       best_t=0; best_s=M; f_prev=f
       for t in [1e-4,1e-3,0.01,0.03,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0]:
          ft=(1-t)*f_prev + t*g_scaled
          s=_score(ft)
          if s < best_s: best_s=s; best_t=t
       if best_t >0:
          # refine around best_t
          low = max(0.0, best_t-0.1); high=min(1.0,best_t+0.1)
          for t in np.linspace(low, high, 9): ...
       if best_s < M*(1-1e-10):
           f = (1-best_t)*f_prev + best_t*g_scaled
           M = best_s
           g = g_scaled
           last_improve=time.monotonic()
       else:
           # maybe try direct g if score lower? included t=1.
           # decrease budget? break
           break
       if it % 20 ==0: print progress
    return f, M
```
But if line search no improve because best_t=0, break. Could be due direction not good; maybe try different eta in approximate or exact LP. We can in loop not break immediately; set no_improve counter, increase dir_time, try. If no_improve>=3 break.

Let's write functions exact:
```
def _conv_general(a,b):
    na=len(a); nb=len(b); need=na+nb-1
    N=_next_pow2(need)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); B=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*B,N)[:need]
    c[np.abs(c)<1e-12]=0.0
    return c
```
For performance, if `b` is None square. Use square for scoring.

Let's ensure our approximate best response update actually implements SOTA. SOTA LP maximizes sum g under f*g ≤ M. Our approximate best response uses MPW to solve that LP. Let's refine MPW to ensure it produces a g with sum >T:
- Start x=f (sum T, max tight). The MPW update with exp(-eta h) then scale to tight. For small eta, shape perturbation; sum after scaling = T * (sum exp(-eta h_center/scale))? Since centering removes mean, sum of exp approx 1 + eta² Var(h)/2 >1, so scaling to tight (which may have max <1? If constraint max remains same maybe scale >1) should increase sum. Good. For small eta, it's a second-order increase if direction has variance and doesn't increase causing scale down too much. Thus should find improvement.

What if max constraint after shape update is the same row as before (so scale same? Actually scale to tight based on max vy; if max row still the same and its value decreased (because we reduced variables contributing to it), then scale up increases sum. If max row was not decreased enough, scale factor may be <1? But we scale always to make max=1; if y before scaling has max <M, scale up. If max >M, scale down. Since exp reduces high-h variables, likely max of active constraints decreases, so scale up. Good.

`_next_pow2(m)`:
```
def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    return 1 << int(np.ceil(np.log2(m))) # but np.log2 maybe; use bit_length:
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()
```
For m Python int. Use:
```
def _next_pow2(m):
    m=int(m)
    if m<=1: return 1
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```

`_safe_normalize(p, target=1.0)`:
```
p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
p=np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
s=p.sum()
if s<=0: return np.full(len(p), target/len(p)) if target>0 else p
return p*(target/s)
```
If target=0? not.

`_central(n)`:
```
c=np.empty(n); c[0]=1.0
for i in range(1,n): c[i]=c[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)
return c
```
Use Python loop 2535 fine. For n=8192 fine.

`_conv_square(p)`:
```
p=np.asarray(p,float)
n=p.size
if n==0: return np.array([0.0])
N=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
C=np.fft.irfft(P*P,N)[:2*n-1]
C[~np.isfinite(C)]=0.0
C=np.maximum(C,0.0) # small negatives? For max okay. But if clipping negatives to zero, no effect on max; for C in weights, zero.
return C
```
Clipping all negatives to 0 could hide tiny negative but okay. Might slightly affect sum? Not used.

`_conv_general(a,b)`:
```
need=len(a)+len(b)-1
N=_next_pow2(need)
A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); B=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
C=np.fft.irfft(A*B,N)[:need]
C[~np.isfinite(C)]=0; C=np.maximum(C,0)
return C
```

`_score(p)`:
```
p=np.asarray(p,float); n=p.size
if n==0: return inf
s=p.sum()
if not finite or s < 1e-12: return inf
C=_conv_square(p); m=float(np.max(C))
if not finite or m <0: return inf
return float(2.0*n*m/(s*s))
```

`_grad_weights(p,w)`:
```
n=len(p); need=2*n-1; N=_next_pow2(3*n-1)
Pr=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], N)
W=np.fft.rfft(w, N) # w length 2n-1
G=np.fft.irfft(Pr*W, N)
g=G[n-1:2*n-1]
g[~finite]=0
return g
```
Check w length exactly 2n-1. In smooth, w normalized C length 2n-1. In approximate, viol length 2n-1.

`_compute_C_g(p,q)`:
```
C=_conv_square(p); M=float(np.max(C)); if M<=0: return C,None,M
r=np.clip(C/M,0.0,1.0)
# To avoid all zeros where r=0 and q>1? okay.
w = np.power(r, q)
sw=w.sum(); if sw<=0: w=C/M; sw=...
w/=sw
g=_grad_weights(p,w)
return C,g,M
```
If q=1, w=C/M; since C may have zeros, okay.

`_smooth_step(p,g,eta)`:
```
gm=g-g.mean(); sc=np.max(np.abs(gm)); if sc<=0: return p.copy()
y=p*np.exp(-eta*gm/sc)
y=np.maximum(y,1e-300)
s=y.sum(); if s<=0: return p.copy()
return y/s
```

`_smooth_polish(p, budget, rng)`:
Need rng passed as np Generator.
```
p=_safe_normalize(p,1.0)
best=p.copy(); bs=_score(p)
start=time.monotonic(); no=0
q_cycle=[1,2,3,4,6,9,14,22,36]
eta_cycle=[0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.8,1.2,2.0]

while time < start+budget and no<40:
   q=q_cycle[qi % len]
   C,g,M=_compute_C_g(best,q)
   if g is None: break
   improved=False
   for eta in eta_cycle:
       cand=_smooth_step(best,g,eta)
       s=_score(cand)
       if s < bs*(1-1e-12):
          best=cand; bs=s; improved=True; no=0; break
   if not improved:
       no +=1; qi +=1
       # occasional blend central
       if no in (5,12,25):
          c=_central(len(best)); c=_safe_normalize(c,1.0)
          for mix in [0.1,0.3,0.5]:
             cand=(1-mix)*best + mix*c; cand=_safe_normalize(cand,1.0); s=_score(cand)
             if s<bs: best=cand; bs=s; no=0; improved=True; break
       if no>=40: break
   if time ...
return best, bs
```
But qi increments each q; if no improved for many, change q. Good.

`_local_polish(p, budget, rng)`:
```
best=_safe_normalize(p,1); bs=_score(best); start=...
n=len(best)
widths=[max(1,int(n*frac)) for frac in (0.002,0.008,0.03,0.1)]
sigmas=[0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.7]
no=0
while time and no<100:
   w=rng.choice(widths) # need seed? rng.choice returns int if array. Use int(rng.integers(0,len(widths))).
   si=rng.integers(0,len(sigmas)); sig=sigmas[si]
   start_i=int(rng.integers(0,max(1,n-w+1)))
   cand=best.copy()
   cand[start_i:start_i+w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, size=w))
   # occasional add small noise to all
   if rng.random()<0.1:
       cand *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig*0.2, size=n))
   cand=_safe_normalize(cand,1.0)
   s=_score(cand)
   if s<bs*(1-1e-12): best=cand; bs=s; no=0
   else: no+=1; 
   if no%50==0: sigmas...? not needed
return best,bs
```
`rng.choice` on list of ints may return numpy scalar; okay but to avoid dependency, use indices.

Exact LP:
```
def _lp_direction(f, M, time_limit):
   if not _HAVE_SCIPY: return None
   n=len(f)
   if n > 1500: # maybe skip to avoid huge?
       return None
   try:
      diags_list=[np.full(n, float(f[d]), dtype=np.float64) for d in range(n)]
      offsets=list(range(n))
      A=_sparse_diags(diags_list, offsets, shape=(2*n-1, n), format='csr')
      b = float(M) * (1.0 + 1e-9) + 1e-12
      b_vec=np.full(2*n-1, b, dtype=np.float64)
      c_vec=-np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
      opts={'presolve': True, 'output_flag': 0}
      try: opts['time_limit']=float(max(1.0,time_limit))
      res=_linprog_func(c_vec, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_vec, bounds=(0, np.inf), method='highs', options=opts)
      if res is not None and res.x is not None:
         g=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
         g=np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
         g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
         # check feasible via conv
         if g.sum()>0:
            v=_conv_general(f,g)
            if v.size and float(np.max(v)) <= b*(1.0+1e-5):
                return g
   except Exception:
       pass
   return None
```

```
def _approx_best_response(f, M, warm, max_time):
   n=len(f); T=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
   if warm is None or len(warm)!=n:
      x=np.asarray(f, dtype=float).copy()
   else:
      x=np.asarray(warm, dtype=float).copy()
   x=np.where(np.isfinite(x),x,0); x=np.maximum(x,0)
   if x.sum()<=0: x=f.copy()
   start=time.monotonic()
   # scale to tight feasible
   viol=_conv_general(f,x)
   maxv=float(np.max(viol)) if viol.size else 0.0
   if maxv <= 1e-300:
       x=f.copy(); viol=_conv_general(f,x); maxv=float(np.max(viol))
   scale = M / maxv  # so conv/M max 1
   x = x * scale
   viol = viol * scale  # now max=M
   # convert to dimensionless V = viol / M, max 1
   V = viol / M
   best_x=x.copy(); best_sum=float(x.sum())
   eta=0.5; no=0; last_best_sum=0
   while time.monotonic()-start < max_time and no < 30:
       # H = (1/M)*grad(f, V)
       h=_grad_weights(f, V)
       h = h / float(M)
       h = h - np.mean(h)
       sc=float(np.max(np.abs(h)))
       if sc <= 1e-300: break
       # Try a few eta? We can inside loop pick eta.
       y = x * np.exp(-eta * (h/sc))
       y = np.maximum(y, 1e-300)
       # scale y to tight
       vy=_conv_general(f,y)
       maxvy=float(np.max(vy))
       if maxvy <= 1e-300: break
       y = y * (M / maxvy)
       ysum=float(y.sum())
       # ensure finite
       if np.isfinite(ysum) and ysum > best_sum*(1+1e-10):
           best_x=y; best_sum=ysum; x=y; no=0
           eta = min(eta*1.15, 5.0)
       else:
           no += 1
           if eta > 0.2:
               eta *= 0.6
           else:
               eta = min(eta*1.5, 5.0)
           x = best_x.copy()
           # recompute V for x
           vx=_conv_general(f,x)
           maxvx=float(np.max(vx))
           if maxvx>0: V = vx / M; V = V / np.max(V)
           if no >= 25: break
   if best_sum > T*(1+1e-8):
       return best_x
   return None
```
Wait when x=best_x, its constraints are tight (max=M), so V max=1. Good.

Potential bug: In the first scaling `scale=M/maxv` where maxv is conv(f,x) max. If warm x sum not T, this scaling may scale down/up to make max=M. If maxv < M, scale>1 increases sum. Good.

In loop, after y scale, `V` for current x is tight. But if we accept y, next iteration uses x=y and V should be conv(f,y)/M. We can compute it now to avoid recompute next? We already computed vy and maxvy; `V = vy / maxvy` (since maxvy scaled to M, so V=vy/M? Actually before scaling y0, vy0 max=maxvy0. y=y0*M/maxvy0. conv(f,y)=vy0*M/maxvy0, max=M. So V = conv/M = vy0 / maxvy0. We can set V = vy / maxvy if we store vy before scaling. In code above, we compute vy, maxvy, then y = y*M/maxvy. We can set V = vy / maxvy. Then next loop h uses V. For rejected x=best_x, recompute. This saves conv. Let's implement:

```

def _sota_loop(p, budget, allow_lp=False):
   n=len(p); T=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
   f=_safe_normalize(p,T)
   M=_score(f)
   g=f.copy()
   start=time.monotonic(); no_improve=0; it=0; used_exact=False
   while time.monotonic()-start < budget:
      rem = budget - (time.monotonic()-start)
      if rem < 3: break
      g_new=None
      if allow_lp and not _lp_too_slow and (it % 4 == 0) and rem > 45 and n <= 1024:
          tlim = min(30.0, rem*0.3)
          t0=time.monotonic()
          g_new=_lp_direction(f,M,tlim)
          dt=time.monotonic()-t0
          if g_new is None and dt > tlim*0.9: _lp_too_slow maybe
      if g_new is None:
          dir_time = min(10.0, max(0.5, rem*0.35))
          if no_improve>0: dir_time *=1.5
          g_new=_approx_best_response(f,M,g,dir_time)
      it +=1
      if g_new is None:
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve >= 4: break
          # try exact if possible and not used
          if allow_lp and rem > 60 and not used_exact and n<=1024:
             g_new=_lp_direction(f,M,min(60,rem*0.4)); used_exact=True
          continue
      s=sum(g_new)
      if s <= T*(1+1e-8):
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve >=4: break
          continue
      g_scaled = g_new * (T/s)
      best_t=0; best_s=M; f_old=f
      # line search
      ts=[1e-4,1e-3,3e-3,1e-2,3e-2,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0]
      for t in ts: ...
      if best_t >0:
         lo=max(0.0, best_t-0.08); hi=min(1.0,best_t+0.08)
         for k in range(9): t=lo + (hi-lo)*k/8 ...
      if best_s < M*(1-1e-10):
          f = (1-best_t)*f_old + best_t*g_scaled
          M=best_s
          g=g_scaled
          no_improve=0
          print progress maybe
      else:
          # maybe direct g score? t=1 included. Try a perturbed g? reduce dir?
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve >=4: break
          # mix g with f using random t? Already line search. Maybe try g normalized p? no.
   return f, M
```

def _parametric_p(n, alpha, beta, right_weight, const_weight):
   i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
   left=i+1.0
   right=n-i
   p=np.power(left, -alpha)
   if right_weight>0: p = p + right_weight * np.power(right, -beta)
   if const_weight>0: p = p + const_weight
   p=np.maximum(p,0); p=_safe_normalize(p,1.0); return p
```

def _resize_sequence(p, new_n):
   m=len(p)
   if m==new_n: return p.copy()
   x_old=np.arange(m, dtype=float)
   x_new=np.linspace(0.0, m-1.0, new_n)
   new=np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
   return _safe_normalize(new,1.0)
```

`_safe_normalize`: If target is 1.0 default. If target small? Use target float.
```
def _safe_normalize(p, target=1.0):
    arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        if arr.size == 0:
            arr = np.ones(8, dtype=np.float64)
        if target > 0:
            arr = np.full(arr.size, target / arr.size, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            arr = np.full(arr.size, 1.0 / arr.size, dtype=np.float64)
        return arr
    return arr * (float(target) / s)
```

`_central`: For n=1, c[0]=1. For n large, factor uses int.
```
def _central(n):
    n=int(n)
    c=np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    c[0]=1.0
    for i in range(1,n):
        c[i] = c[i-1] * ((2*i -1)/(2*i))
    return c
```

`_conv_square`:
```
def _conv_square(p):
    arr=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=arr.size
    if n==0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    N=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    P=np.fft.rfft(arr, N)
    C=np.fft.irfft(P*P, N)[:2*n-1]
    C[~np.isfinite(C)] = 0.0
    C = np.maximum(C, 0.0)
    return C
```

`_conv_general(a,b)`.

`_score`: If `max_c` maybe 0 due all zeros; return inf.

`_grad_weights`: Ensure w size 2n-1; if not, pad/trim? Could assert but avoid. Write:
```
if w.size != 2*n-1:
    tmp=np.zeros(2*n-1,dtype=float)
    m=min(w.size,tmp.size); tmp[:m]=w[:m]; w=tmp

    except NameError: hs=None

       if finite and s<best_score: best_p=p; best_score=s
    print initial best

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative step-function sequence minimizing:

    score(s) = 2*n*max(full_convolution(s,s)) / sum(s)**2

The program combines:
  - central-binomial prefix initialization (gives score ~ pi/2),
  - a resolution-ladder SOTA-style best-response loop,
  - multiplicative-weight approximation of the linear-programming best response,
  - optional exact LP directions via HiGHS/SciPy for small/medium n,
  - smooth max-type polishing and random local search.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog as _linprog
    from scipy.sparse import diags as _diags
    _HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    _linprog = None
    _diags = None
    _HAVE_SCIPY = False

_MAX_EXACT_N = 1024
_TINY = 1e-300
_CAP_FACTOR = 1000.0


def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _safe_normalize(p, target=1.0):
    arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        arr = np.ones(16, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    target = float(target)
    if s <= _TINY:
        if target > 0:
            arr = np.full(arr.size, target / max(1, arr.size), dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            arr = np.full(arr.size, 1.0 / max(1, arr.size), dtype=np.float64)
        return arr
    return arr * (target / s)


def _central(n):
    n = int(n)
    c = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    c[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        c[i] = c[i - 1] * ((2 * i - 1) / (2 * i))
    return c


def _conv_square(p):
    arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = arr.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    N = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    P = np.fft.rfft(arr, N)
    C = np.fft.irfft(P * P, N)[:2 * n - 1]
    C[~np.isfinite(C)] = 0.0
    C = np.maximum(C, 0.0)
    return C


def _conv_general(a, b):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    need = len(a) + len(b) - 1
    if need <= 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    N = _next_pow2(need)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    B = np.fft.rfft(b, N)
    C = np.fft.irfft(A * B, N)[:need]
    C[~np.isfinite(C)] = 0.0
    C = np.maximum(C, 0.0)
    return C


def _score(p):
    arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = arr.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return float("inf")
    C = _conv_square(arr)
    m = float(np.max(C)) if C.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def _grad_weights(p, w):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    need = 2 * n - 1
    if w.size != need:
        tmp = np.zeros(need, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(w.size, need)
        tmp[:m] = w[:m]
        w = tmp
    N = _next_pow2(3 * n - 1)
    Pr = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], N)
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    G = np.fft.irfft(Pr * W, N)
    g = G[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    g[~np.isfinite(g)] = 0.0
    return g


def _compute_C_g(p, q):
    C = _conv_square(p)
    M = float(np.max(C)) if C.size else 0.0
    if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return C, None, M
    r = np.clip(C / M, _TINY, 1.0)
    q = max(1.0, float(q))
    if q > 80.0:
        q = 80.0
    w = np.power(r, q)
    sw = float(w.sum())
    if sw <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(sw):
        w = C / M
        sw = float(w.sum())
        if sw <= 0.0:
            w = np.ones_like(C)
            sw = float(w.size)
    w = w / sw
    g = _grad_weights(p, w)
    return C, g, M


def _smooth_step(p, g, eta):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if g.size != p.size:
        return p.copy()
    gm = g - float(np.mean(g))
    sc = float(np.max(np.abs(gm)))
    if sc <= _TINY:
        return p.copy()
    y = p * np.exp(-float(eta) * (gm / sc))
    y = np.maximum(y, _TINY)
    s = float(y.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return p.copy()
    return y / s


def _smooth_polish(p, budget, rng):
    p = _safe_normalize(p, 1.0)
    best = p.copy()
    best_s = _score(best)
    if budget <= 0 or rng is None:
        return best, best_s
    start = time.monotonic()
    no_improve = 0
    qi = 0
    q_cycle = [1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 14, 22, 36]
    eta_cycle = [0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5, 0.8, 1.2, 2.0]

    while time.monotonic() - start < float(budget) and no_improve < 45:
        q = q_cycle[qi % len(q_cycle)]
        C, g, M = _compute_C_g(best, q)
        if g is None:
            break
        improved = False
        for eta in eta_cycle:
            cand = _smooth_step(best, g, eta)
            s = _score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s * (1.0 - 1e-12):
                best = cand
                best_s = s
                improved = True
                no_improve = 0
                break
        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            qi += 1
            if no_improve in (5, 12, 24):
                c = _safe_normalize(_central(best.size), 1.0)
                for mix in (0.1, 0.25, 0.4):
                    cand = (1.0 - mix) * best + mix * c
                    cand = _safe_normalize(cand, 1.0)
                    s = _score(cand)
                    if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s:
                        best = cand
                        best_s = s
                        improved = True
                        no_improve = 0
                        break
        if time.monotonic() - start > float(budget):
            break
    return best, best_s


def _local_polish(p, budget, rng):
    p = _safe_normalize(p, 1.0)
    best = p.copy()
    best_s = _score(best)
    if budget <= 0 or rng is None or best.size == 0:
        return best, best_s
    n = best.size
    widths = [max(1, int(n * fr)) for fr in (0.002, 0.008, 0.03, 0.10)]
    sigmas = [0.05, 0.10, 0.18, 0.35, 0.65]
    start = time.monotonic()
    no_improve = 0
    while time.monotonic() - start < float(budget) and no_improve < 200:
        wi = int(rng.integers(0, len(widths)))
        si = int(rng.integers(0, len(sigmas)))
        w = widths[wi]
        sigma = sigmas[si]
        if w > n:
            w = n
        start_i = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - w + 1)))
        cand = best.copy()
        cand[start_i:start_i + w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=w))
        if rng.random() < 0.12:
            cand *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25 * sigma, size=n))
        cand = _safe_normalize(cand, 1.0)
        s = _score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s * (1.0 - 1e-12):
            best = cand
            best_s = s
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
    return best, best_s


def _parametric_p(n, alpha, beta, right_weight, const_weight):
    i = np.arange(int(n), dtype=np.float64)
    left = i + 1.0
    right = int(n) - i
    p = np.power(left, -float(alpha))
    if float(right_weight) > 0.0:
        p = p + float(right_weight) * np.power(right, -float(beta))
    if float(const_weight) > 0.0:
        p = p + float(const_weight)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    return _safe_normalize(p, 1.0)


def _resize_sequence(p, new_n):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    new_n = int(new_n)
    if p.size == 0 or new_n <= 0:
        return _safe_normalize(_central(max(1, new_n)), 1.0)
    if p.size == new_n:
        return _safe_normalize(p, 1.0)
    x_old = np.arange(p.size, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, p.size - 1.0, new_n, dtype=np.float64)
    new = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
    return _safe_normalize(new, 1.0)


def _lp_direction(f, M, time_limit):
    if not _HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    n = len(f)
    if n > _MAX_EXACT_N or n <= 0:
        return None
    try:
        diags_list = []
        offsets = []
        for d in range(n):
            val = float(f[d])
            if abs(val) > 1e-300:
                diags_list.append(np.full(n, val, dtype=np.float64))
                offsets.append(d)
        if not diags_list:
            return None
        A = _diags(diags_list, offsets, shape=(2 * n - 1, n), format="csr")
        b = max(float(M), 1e-300) * (1.0 + 1e-9) + 1e-12
        b_vec = np.full(2 * n - 1, b, dtype=np.float64)
        c_vec = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
        opts = {"presolve": True, "output_flag": 0}
        try:
            opts["time_limit"] = max(1.0, float(time_limit))
        except Exception:
            return None
        res = _linprog(
            c_vec,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_vec,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options=opts,
        )
        if res is not None and getattr(res, "x", None) is not None:
            g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
            g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
            if g.sum() > 0.0:
                v = _conv_general(f, g)
                if v.size and float(np.max(v)) <= b * (1.0 + 1e-5):
                    return g
    except Exception:
        return None
    return None


def _approx_best_response(f, M, warm, max_time, rng):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = f.size
    M = float(M)
    if n <= 0 or M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return None
    T = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))

    if warm is None or len(warm) != n:
        x = f.copy()
    else:
        x = np.asarray(warm, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
    if x.sum() <= _TINY:
        x = f.copy()

    start = time.monotonic()
    viol = _conv_general(f, x)
    maxv = float(np.max(viol)) if viol.size else 0.0
    if maxv <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(maxv):
        x = f.copy()
        viol = _conv_general(f, x)
        maxv = float(np.max(viol))
    factor = M / maxv
    if factor > _CAP_FACTOR:
        factor = _CAP_FACTOR
    x = x * factor
    viol = viol * factor
    V = viol / M
    mx = float(np.max(V)) if V.size else 0.0
    if mx > 0.0:
        V = V / mx

    best_x = x.copy()
    best_sum = float(x.sum())
    best_V = V.copy()
    eta = 0.5
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() - start < float(max_time) and no_improve < 35:
        h = _grad_weights(f, V) / M
        h = h - float(np.mean(h))
        sc = float(np.max(np.abs(h)))
        if sc <= _TINY:
            break

        y = x * np.exp(-eta * (h / sc))
        y = np.maximum(y, _TINY)
        vy = _conv_general(f, y)
        maxvy = float(np.max(vy)) if vy.size else 0.0
        if maxvy <= _TINY or not np.isfinite(maxvy):
            break
        fac = M / maxvy
        if fac > _CAP_FACTOR:
            fac = _CAP_FACTOR
        y = y * fac
        V_new = (vy / M) * fac
        mnew = float(np.max(V_new)) if V_new.size else 0.0
        if mnew > 0.0:
            V_new = V_new / mnew
        ysum = float(y.sum())
        if np.isfinite(ysum) and ysum > best_sum * (1.0 + 1e-10):
            best_x = y
            best_sum = ysum
            best_V = V_new
            x = y
            V = V_new
            no_improve = 0
            eta = min(eta * 1.15, 5.0)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            x = best_x.copy()
            V = best_V.copy()
            if eta > 0.25:
                eta *= 0.65
            else:
                eta = min(eta * 1.6, 5.0)

            if no_improve >= 3 and rng is not None and (no_improve % 3 == 0):
                yy = x * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.12, size=n))
                yy = np.maximum(yy, _TINY)
                vyy = _conv_general(f, yy)
                maxy = float(np.max(vyy)) if vyy.size else 0.0
                if maxy > _TINY and np.isfinite(maxy):
                    fac2 = min(M / maxy, _CAP_FACTOR)
                    yy = yy * fac2
                    Vn = (vyy / M) * fac2
                    mn = float(np.max(Vn)) if Vn.size else 0.0
                    if mn > 0.0:
                        Vn = Vn / mn
                    s2 = float(yy.sum())
                    if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 > best_sum * (1.0 + 1e-10):
                        best_x = yy
                        best_sum = s2
                        best_V = Vn
                        x = yy
                        V = Vn
                        no_improve = 0
                        eta = min(eta * 1.2, 5.0)
            if no_improve >= 30:
                break

    if best_sum > T * (1.0 + 1e-7) and np.isfinite(best_sum):
        return best_x
    return None


def _sota_loop(p, budget, allow_lp, rng):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n <= 0 or budget <= 0:
        return p, _score(p)
    T = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    f = _safe_normalize(p, T)
    M = _score(f)
    g = f.copy()
    start = time.monotonic()
    dir_base = 2.0
    no_improve = 0
    iters = 0

    while time.monotonic() - start < float(budget):
        rem = float(budget) - (time.monotonic() - start)
        if rem < 2.0:
            break

        g_new = None
        if allow_lp and n <= _MAX_EXACT_N and rem > 35.0:
            tlim = min(30.0, rem * 0.35)
            t0 = time.monotonic()
            g_new = _lp_direction(f, M, tlim)
            if g_new is not None:
                pass
            elif time.monotonic() - t0 > tlim * 0.9 and rem < 200.0:
                allow_lp = False

        if g_new is None:
            dir_time = min(20.0, max(1.0, dir_base))
            g_new = _approx_best_response(f, M, g, dir_time, rng)

        iters += 1
        s_g = float(np.sum(g_new)) if g_new is not None else 0.0
        if g_new is None or not np.isfinite(s_g) or s_g <= T * (1.0 + 1e-7):
            no_improve += 1
            dir_base = max(1.0, dir_base * 0.85)
            if no_improve >= 5:
                break
            continue

        g_scaled = g_new * (T / s_g)
        g_scaled = _safe_normalize(g_scaled, T)

        f_old = f
        best_t = 0.0
        best_s = M
        coarse_t = (1e-4, 1e-3, 3e-3, 1e-2, 3e-2, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0)
        for tt in coarse_t:
            if tt > 1.0:
                continue
            ft = (1.0 - tt) * f_old + tt * g_scaled
            ss = _score(ft)
            if np.isfinite(ss) and ss < best_s:
                best_s = ss
                best_t = tt

        if best_t > 0.0:
            lo = max(0.0, best_t - 0.10)
            hi = min(1.0, best_t + 0.10)
            for k in range(11):
                tt = lo + (hi - lo) * (float(k) / 10.0)
                ft = (1.0 - tt) * f_old + tt * g_scaled
                ss = _score(ft)
                if np.isfinite(ss) and ss < best_s:
                    best_s = ss
                    best_t = tt

        if np.isfinite(best_s) and best_s < M * (1.0 - 1e-10):
            f = f_old + best_t * (g_scaled - f_old)
            oldM = M
            M = best_s
            g = g_scaled
            no_improve = 0
            dir_base = min(dir_base * 1.2, 20.0)
            if (iters % 5 == 0) or (oldM - M) > 1e-4:
                print(
                    "SOTA n=%d iter=%d score=%.8f mix=%.4g" % (n, iters, M, best_t),
                    flush=True,
                )
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            dir_base = max(1.0, dir_base * 0.75)
            if no_improve >= 5:
                break

    return f, M


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    total_budget = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    start = time.monotonic()
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_p = None
    best_score = float("inf")
    best_n = 0

    def _consider(p, name=""):
        nonlocal best_p, best_score, best_n
        if p is None:
            return
        p = _safe_normalize(p, 1.0)
        if p.size == 0:
            return
        s = _score(p)
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
            best_p = p
            best_score = s
            best_n = p.size
            print("candidate %s n=%d score=%.8f" % (name, p.size, s), flush=True)

    # Initial candidates: central prefix constructions at several sizes.
    for n0 in (512, 1024, 2048, 2535, 4096):
        c = _safe_normalize(_central(n0), 1.0)
        _consider(c, "central")

    # Existing SOTA sequence if available.
    try:
        hs = height_sequence_1
    except Exception:
        hs = None
    if hs is not None:
        _consider(hs, "height_sequence_1")

    if best_p is None:
        _consider(_central(2535), "fallback central")

    # Small parametric search to provide diversified starts.
    param_budget = min(80.0, total_budget * 0.04)
    param_start = time.monotonic()
    for n0 in (1024, 2048, 2535):
        if time.monotonic() - param_start > param_budget:
            break
        a = 0.5
        b = 0.0
        rw = 0.0
        cw = 0.0
        for _ in range(250):
            if time.monotonic() - param_start > param_budget:
                break
            a = float(np.clip(a + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05), 0.05, 1.10))
            b = float(np.clip(b + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05), 0.0, 1.10))
            rw = float(np.clip(rw + rng.normal(0.0, 0.7), 0.0, 10.0))
            cw = float(np.clip(cw + rng.normal(0.0, 0.12), 0.0, 2.0))
            p = _parametric_p(n0, a, b, rw, cw)
            s = _score(p)
            if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score * 1.0:
                # keep only if clearly competitive; _consider prints.
                if s < best_score:
                    _consider(p, "parametric")
                else:
                    _consider(p, "parametric-line")

    # Main resolution ladder.  Each stage runs the SOTA-style loop on an
    # upsampled version of the current best, then records the best global result.
    specs = [
        (256, 45),
        (512, 90),
        (1024, 190),
        (2048, 260),
        (2535, 1400),
        (4096, 180),
    ]

    current_p = None
    for n0, alloc in specs:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        remaining = total_budget - elapsed
        if remaining < 140.0:
            break

        init_list = []
        init_list.append(_safe_normalize(_central(n0), 1.0))
        if current_p is not None:
            init_list.append(_resize_sequence(current_p, n0))
        if best_p is not None:
            init_list.append(_resize_sequence(best_p, n0))
        if n0 == 2535:
            try:
                hs = height_sequence_1
                init_list.append(_safe_normalize(hs, 1.0))
            except Exception:
                pass

        best_init = None
        best_init_s = float("inf")
        for ic in init_list:
            if ic is None or len(ic) == 0:
                continue
            s = _score(ic)
            if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_init_s:
                best_init_s = s
                best_init = ic
        if best_init is None:
            best_init = init_list[0]

        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        stage_budget = min(float(alloc), max(8.0, (total_budget - elapsed) * 0.50))
        # Leave time for final polish and official evaluation.
        cap = max(8.0, (total_budget - elapsed) - 120.0)
        stage_budget = min(stage_budget, cap)
        if stage_budget <= 0.0:
            break

        f0 = _safe_normalize(best_init, np.sqrt(2.0 * n0))
        allow_lp = _HAVE_SCIPY and (n0 <= _MAX_EXACT_N)
        f_new, M_new = _sota_loop(f0, stage_budget, allow_lp, rng)
        p_new = _safe_normalize(f_new, 1.0)
        s_new = _score(p_new)
        _consider(p_new, "sota")

        # Keep the better of stage result and the current best projected to n0.
        if best_p is not None:
            p_proj = _resize_sequence(best_p, n0)
            sp = _score(p_proj)
            if np.isfinite(sp) and sp < (s_new if np.isfinite(s_new) else float("inf")):
                current_p = p_proj
            else:
                current_p = p_new
        else:
            current_p = p_new

    # Final polishing on the global best.
    remaining = total_budget - (time.monotonic() - start)
    if best_p is not None and remaining > 80.0:
        smooth_budget = min(300.0, max(20.0, remaining * 0.40))
        p_sm, s_sm = _smooth_polish(best_p, smooth_budget, rng)
        if np.isfinite(s_sm) and s_sm < best_score:
            best_p = p_sm
            best_score = s_sm
            print("smooth final score=%.8f" % best_score, flush=True)

    remaining = total_budget - (time.monotonic() - start)
    if best_p is not None and remaining > 80.0:
        local_budget = min(360.0, max(20.0, remaining * 0.50))
        p_loc, s_loc = _local_polish(best_p, local_budget, rng)
        if np.isfinite(s_loc) and s_loc < best_score:
            best_p = p_loc
            best_score = s_loc
            print("local final score=%.8f" % best_score, flush=True)

    # Ensure a valid sequence is returned.
    final_p = _safe_normalize(best_p, 1.0)
    final_p = np.clip(final_p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    final_p = np.where(np.isfinite(final_p), final_p, 0.0)
    if final_p.size == 0:
        final_p = _safe_normalize(_central(2535), 1.0)

    try:
        official = float(evaluate_sequence(final_p.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        official = float("inf")

    if not np.isfinite(official) or official > best_score * 1.2:
        fallback = _safe_normalize(_central(2535), 1.0)
        fallback = np.clip(fallback, 0.0, 1000.0)
        try:
            official_fb = float(evaluate_sequence(fallback.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            official_fb = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(official_fb) and official_fb < official:
            final_p = fallback
            official = official_fb

    print("FINAL internal=%.8f official=%.8f n=%d" % (_score(final_p), official, final_p.size), flush=True)
    return [float(x) for x in final_p]
```
